# The canonical Conduit lifecycle journey

Status: in progress, 4 November 2026. This chronological account is an executable
delivery record, not a claim that the destination below is already implemented.

## Destination and invariants

An existing authoring Host runs Crèche, births a Body, and provides ordinary
authoring and checking of Host definitions in Conduitese. Crèche accepts a saved
`.host.conduit` definition or supports creating one, resolves its construction,
and uses shared Make to produce the destination artifacts. The provisional
user-facing word **Seed** refers to the existing Spore construction family; this
record does not change a wire schema or claim that provisional vocabulary is a
new architecture.

Crèche stays on the authoring Host. It may offer an admitted flashing, transfer
or installation carrier, or retain artifacts for an explicitly recorded human
transfer. The destination executes its prepared realization and joins the same
once-born Body through actual invitation/membership. Making an artifact, writing
it, observing a Boot and admitting a member remain separate outcomes.

The Body's ordinary Plots then compose sensing, retained history, computation
and human interaction. One coherent semantic Face supplies multiple Masks;
checked Looks may be reused across those Masks without changing the underlying
quantities, actions, provenance or failures. Device-specific provider/wiring
truth remains in Host construction and actual initialized offers.

## Milestone acceptance

| Stage | Required witness | Current result |
|---|---|---|
| Existing Host and Crèche | Actual installed Host/Boot and acknowledged encounter | Verified installed Linux service, Birth encounter and subsequent Crèche encounter |
| Birth with useful tools | One reviewed birth, retained Source/workset, installed authoring/Make capabilities | Hamm Creek House born; checked Host authoring and Crèche used; full toolset remains limited |
| Wake and next Seed | Same Body, authored/checked Host definition, admitted Make and exact artifact | Body-authored timer and Pico joining Seeds verified; complete graphical successor remains open |
| Deployment and admission | Explicit carrier or human transfer, actual new Boot and authorized same-Body member | Physical Threebee timer and Green Pico Seed Boot/admission verified; graphical deployment pending |
| Grow | Same recognizable path for RP2040, ESP32, Pi and browser | Not achieved |
| Continue and present | Sensing/recording/interaction under exact Plans/Lines; coherent actual Faces/Shows | Not achieved |
| Reproduce | Source, commands, chronological receipts, images and physical evidence | This record begins that evidence; whole journey remains open |

A smaller fixture, a manually assembled Rust owner, a caption rename or copied
BodyId cannot satisfy those stages. Extract a shared mechanism only when the
next actual stage demonstrates its need. Existing issues own the scope; no
parallel environmental framework is introduced.

## 1. Entrance inventory and first real refusal

Source inspected: `dc2e85c6e2cf9ab54610885206ac2ff925b7808e`. Forebrain's existing
default installation retained no Body and named Host
`host/installed/f0ccc266877e6d1c192e22246ddaef8be554c52c408a6a8bcbc967c1b1775ed8`,
from older release source `a3c08207090d931f9090831629463225a24a8c64`.

Invoking that exact installed executable with:

```sh
conduit body birth --screen-free --state-dir "$HOME/.local/state/conduit"
```

returned exit2, `unrecognized subcommand 'birth'`. The saved runtime marker's
PID5834 actually belonged to `/usr/bin/dbus-daemon`; a marker was not accepted
as evidence that the installed Host was live. No Body was created or reset.
The old Hamm Body and independently recovered readings remain separate retained
custody; they are not repurposed as proof of this new birth.

## 2. Bootstrap the existing authoring Host through Make

The next operation is the supported existing release entrance, from the clean
source above:

```sh
cargo +stable xtask host release --platform linux \
  --output /absolute/private/host-releases \
  --source-identity dc2e85c6e2cf9ab54610885206ac2ff925b7808e --json
```

The Linux release set builds x86_64 and AArch64 executables and sealed target
manifests. This is bootstrap preparation of the preexisting authoring Host, not
a Seed claimed to have been made by a Body that does not yet exist. Completion,
verified installation and the actual Crèche encounter are recorded below.
Raw logs and private receipts are retained outside Git under the task's ignored
`target/canonical-lifecycle-journey-20261101/` output.

### Installed entrance reached

The release job completed successfully for x86_64 and AArch64. The ordinary
`host service install` command verified the sealed x86_64 bundle and activated
the user service. Host identity above was preserved. Actual Boot
`boot/installed/043fc8d265ae6b940dd261bdb0bb21328b17b79992a964c57f7a1939f306fbf0`
reported offer generation1, and its live process executable matched the installed
release path. The bundle digest is
`sha256:e2c12ab001b76b1017bc8c242afbe6bfceeadfc1369a69781ff2434958959c2a`.

Invoking the installed `body birth --screen-free` opened the actual service-owned
Face revision1 with an acknowledged terminal Show. Its available Plots were
Morse Network and Interval ticker. The required authoring/Make tools were absent;
the operator explicitly exited without Birth. This is actual installed terminal
evidence, not a graphical screenshot, useful-tool Body, Seed or membership proof.
Compact receipt: `proof/evidence/creche/installed-birth-entrance-20261101.json`.

The shared Plot editor catalog now checks/expands the shipped Crèche Source;
all four catalog regressions passed. The recovered Std supplied-owner path passed
18 tests again after integration with dev43aae275ce. Exact shipped-source product
Fore planning passed two tests, including refusal without an installed offer and
refusal of a same-name Plot with different Source identity. Those are software
preparation/execution proofs; actual checked Crèche provider selection and the
production encounter owner remain the next installed gates under #6468.

### Checked authoring-Host selection

The hosted Make package now checks an explicit Linux x86_64 `creche/encounter`
Base selection pinned to the existing `std/kernel-creche-prepare@1`
implementation. Required initialization declares the slot identity, evidence
ceiling, poll count and minimum poll interval through the ordinary finite Base
initialization schema. All23 package tests passed, including canonical roundtrip,
identity changes, malformed/missing/duplicate fields, unsupported targets, exact
Host Call/resource/Base prerequisites and dependency-cycle refusal. The slot is
not a byte reservation, build grant, initialized provider or Ready advertisement.

The product planning slice passed its two focused tests and strict binary Clippy;
the combined checked-selection dependency also passed strict binary Clippy.
PR #6706 retains the separate Std execution slice. Its initial quick check found
module ordering; that formatting-only fault was corrected without changing the
qualified execution behavior. The accepted projection-evidence changes from
dev1e1456e4af were then integrated. No new release containing these additions has
been installed yet: the live entrance above remains the pinned dc2e85c6e2 build.

### Shared joining-Host construction

The existing browser Workspace Seed path now uses shared `check_joining_host`
instead of assembling its construction through the browser target template.
The helper validates the retained Body biography and public invitation scope and
expiry, rechecks the exact selected Host configuration/profile against the supplied
catalogue/packages, and produces checked self-joining construction intent. The
existing sealer retains the original Birth Sign and selected image digest.
No new birth, authority, artifact, provider readiness or membership is inferred.

Two shared tests and all nine browser Spore regressions passed, including the
actual Workspace consumer and expired-invitation refusal. Strict library Clippy
and workspace formatting passed. Independent review found no concrete defect.
Construction now uses the Body friendly name and a digest-derived configuration
reference, so new construction/Spore identities may differ from earlier output.
Proof: `proof/evidence/creche/joining-host-construction-20261101.json`.
This is native Rust software proof, not deployed WASM, installed provider, media
manufacture or physical joining; #6471's native media/boot acceptance stays open.

### Bounded Host authoring and same-Body Seed preparation

The next shared tool source is now qualified: `HostConfigurationEncounter`
loads a checked saved definition or lets the current execution author one from
package descriptors. It exposes finite target/Base choices, explicit limits and
initialization, and inspectable canonical Conduitese in bounded text chunks.
Every edit validates the current Face and Show; stale interactions refuse.
Its checked result is construction intent, never provider readiness.

The same package now provides `produce_joining_host` for a prior ordinary Make
artifact explicitly selected and opened by its authorized owner. It streams a
bounded artifact check, checks the invitation again after reading, uses the
original Body/Birth identity, and retains the Seed manifest and receipt. This
observes artifact bytes and retains the handle; the owner still must preserve
immutable custody or verify bytes at deployment. It performs no file opening,
build, deployment, Boot observation or membership admission itself.

Five authoring tests, six production tests and strict library Clippy pass on
source `a564fbf32d7726ebac9cf98697d8e7e147bc72fb`. Exact logs and bounds are in
`make/body/src/host_configuration_encounter/qualification-20261101.json`.
This is software proof, not a deployed UI screenshot or a joined Host.
The existing Linux source builder supports ConduitOS targets only; browser/Pico
builder support must not be inferred from this reviewed-artifact route.

The installed provider must next retain its catalogs, actual backend/resource
custody and factory before initial advertisement. Per-Play construction uses the
actual execution context and the original StdHost; the existing service's Face,
Mask acknowledgment and typed input routes must own presentation. Do not recover
the old another-Body deployment draft, replacement ledger or second native UI.
The related Std integration-harness repair passes strict region integration
Clippy and the exact allocation-free quarantine test; its separate receipt is
`proof/evidence/creche/std-supplied-encounter-harness-20261101.json`.

The retained another-Body image producer is not the per-Host extension path.
Use existing validated biography/invitation Spore sealing to preserve the first

## Independent device maintenance

Threebee's existing useful display was updated separately to local candidate
`153be296f2`, incorporating the current shared development code while retaining
its graph and controls. Actual AArch64 CPU tests passed49 cases, and an actual
retained-history preview passed. Other Host/Face/history processes were preserved
at rollout and a rollback was retained. Its old Body feed still says LAST KNOWN
and FEED DELAYED. That display update is physical renderer maintenance, not the
new journey's Body, live observations, membership, or Look/Show acceptance.

User-requested Look consistency will use existing checked Look resources and
component configuration across applications. Selection defaults/local preference
paths require reconciliation; new application-specific styling or Face truth is
not a replacement. Actual screenshots, artifact hashes and rollback receipts are
retained in the task's Threebee update output; no framebuffer capture is added
to source.

## Checked equipment and programmable composition

Each Host definition must declare its actual physical attachments and selected
Base implementations: for example a DHT11 data GPIO, or a BME280 I2C bus/address.
Checked construction must resolve those selections into the appropriate standard
semantic Gear Kind implementations. A GPIO resource reservation alone is not
sensor support; a checked declaration alone is not an initialized ready offer.
Admission still uses actual initialized providers, finite resources and authority.

The retained Yellow definition already records DHT11 GP3 and TM1637 GP7/GP6 in
typed device declarations. That is useful construction input, not proof that the
whole checked-definition-to-offer-to-Play path is installed. Do not replace this
path with a device-name switch in an environmental owner.

An authored Plot selects/composes the standard observation operations and routes
their typed feeds through admitted Cords/Lines into recording and graph
operations. Threebee realizes the resulting semantic graph through its Mask;
its display process must not secretly assemble or poll an independently defined
environmental application. Feed selection, composition and finite history/window
bounds must be programmable through ordinary checked source. Reuse existing
measurement and graph contracts where their meaning fits; illustrative names
such as `environment/current-temperature` are not new canonical Kind identities.

Keep the operation/value boundary open until the real provider contracts are
examined. The preferred direction is an observe/sample operation producing typed
observations: temperature is a semantic quantity, while observe, sample, publish
and retain describe operations. Do not preselect a temperature-specific Gear
Kind, or claim one general sampling contract can hide different device timing,
failures, available quantities or provenance. Compare existing observation and
quantity contracts before settling the exact Kind and Fore.

For each real device, retain the chain: checked Host configuration → selected
Base/provider implementation → actual ready offer → exact admitted Plan/Play →
typed observation provenance → authored graph contribution → acknowledged Show.
Changing equipment configuration must change construction/admission evidence;
changing a Look must not change this semantic chain.

The revised 1 November objective makes these separate delivery gates, not
optional future improvements:

| Gate | Required evidence | Current result |
|---|---|---|
| Physical configuration | Actual checked attachments and finite Base selections resolved through Host Make | Retained device declarations exist; full installed path unproved |
| Interchangeable semantic providers | Exact common Kind/Fore meaning, device protocol/conversion and initialized ready offer | Must verify per supported sensor; no equivalence inferred from a label |
| Programmable graph | Authored typed feed connections, window/axes/series/unit configuration, admitted generic graph operation and actual Threebee Show | Existing environmental renderer does not satisfy this gate |
| Reuse beyond environment | Same graph contract consumes a compatible non-environmental typed series, respecting clock/provenance/gaps | Not demonstrated |
| Reconfiguration | Appropriate checked construction/Plan/lifecycle boundary for attachment, Plot and presentation changes | No blanket live-reconfiguration claim |

Accepted source already contains `data/measurement-plot` and
`face/measurement-plot` contracts in `semantics/data`, plus bounded window and
measurement operations. Inspect and reuse their exact meaning before extending
them. Their presence alone does not establish configurable axes, cross-Host
delivery, installed execution or actual graphical output.

## Shared presentation conformance

Applications contribute semantic Face content through the shared contracts.
Masks realize that content for their medium; Looks are independently selected,
checked expressions reusable by multiple applications and compatible Masks.
Appearance selection must preserve Face identity/revision, actions, source
quantities, provenance, freshness and required reading order.

The first conformance witness reuses the existing authored Field day/night
resources on Crèche and an unrelated Todo Face, through checked native/browser
Mask configuration. Existing cross-application tests are the starting point,
not a second presentation framework. Record requested and resolved Look identity,
constraints, recipe identity and actual Show evidence. Exercise text scaling,
forced colors and honest unsupported-resource fallback; an old Show must not
authorize an action after presentation changes.

Reconcile the divergent Phosphor/Navy entry defaults and local preference paths
at shared selection boundaries. Do not fix this by duplicating app stylesheets.
Unit preference remains independently retained across Look changes. Limited
displays realize only supported facets: a seven-segment Mask must preserve typed
quantity meaning without acquiring a graphical engine. Terminal presentation
preserves semantic order and actions without claiming visual Look support.

Threebee's local theme/preferences path is not yet ordinary installed Mask/Show
custody. Its successful renderer update must not be counted as this conformance
gate. Existing ownership remains #5368, #5377, #6485–#6487 and #6628; the current
lifecycle milestone determines the next implementation slice.

`proof/evidence/creche/installed-owner-kernel-20261101.json` for exact log hashes.
These are CPU lifecycle tests using fixture artifact bytes. They do not prove
a deployable Seed or a real graphical Show.

Inspection of the retained prior browser Make output exposed the next real
boundary before deployment: its release identity hashes the file inventory,
whereas the new opaque-file producer currently uses the raw artifact SHA as
image content identity. Raw WASM and ZIP hashes cannot replace that aggregate.
The existing `BodyBoundArtifactIdentity` already distinguishes image content
from carrier artifact content. Reuse the existing release verifier and browser
packager, including profile-gated image correspondence and private invitation
provisioning. Do not relabel a file or rebuild merely to hide this mismatch.
The authoring service and existing device deployments remain unchanged at this
checkpoint; the full lifecycle journey remains incomplete.

## Combined bundle production check — 2 November UTC

The target/package primitives were integrated into the installed Crèche. Bundle
providers now require retained, verified payload handles; an unproved raw
NativeBundle/BrowserBundle refuses before readiness. The selected Host Base
uses a pinned existing release manifest and a private `package_directory`.
Artifact capacity is checked before offering the provider; production writes
an unlinked private ZIP, rechecks invitation expiry/cancellation, and retains
bounded evidence separately from its artifact file. This integration is not
yet qualified or deployed.

The first combined library run passed seven tests and refused the successful
production case. Retained evidence showed `native ZIP lost exact release,
Spore or invitation binding`. Inspection found that the package primitive
compared the Spore's Body birth/construction source with the release's compiler
source. The ordinary sealer deliberately preserves the former; artifact
provenance belongs to its Make IMAGE. The correction must accept the complete
BuiltSpore and validate the IMAGE source while preserving the original Body
identity. Earlier primitive reader tests used matching synthetic labels and
did not expose this distinction. Preserve that limit when citing them.

The existing native installer also reconstructs release `source_identity` from
the Spore source. Its format-reader success does not prove correct installed
artifact-source reporting. Keep that downstream correction separate from a
claim of browser Boot or Body membership; neither is demonstrated here.

Source `6fb46b7eab` corrects the identity refusal using the complete BuiltSpore:

checks pass; strict library/binary/test Clippy and formatting pass. Exact logs
and sequencing are recorded in

This is CPU/software and actual-input proof, not live service deployment,
attended Show, browser Boot or membership. The next real boundary is exporting

provenance remains a distinct known limitation. The full goal remains open.

## Authenticated Seed export — 2 November UTC

Source `b56eefe55b` adds the ordinary local entrance
`conduit body export-seed --state-dir PATH DESTINATION`. It uses the existing
authenticated Unix control socket and shared artifact carrier, reading the
owner's actual retained file rather than reconstructing configuration or
provisioning. It refuses during an active Body worker and rejects an expired
invitation before copying. Current Body identity and original Birth Sign are
checked separately from the exact raw ZIP hash and aggregate IMAGE content.
Private atomic output never overwrites an existing destination. Transfer
receipts explicitly leave Boot, joining and membership unproved. A timeout
leaves the outcome unknown; the client does not delete the destination.

Four control/CLI tests, thirteen ordinary Owner/kernel tests (including actual
Seed export), eight adapter tests, eight package tests, strict Clippy and
formatting pass. See `proof/evidence/creche/installed-seed-export-20261102.json`
for exact source/log hashes. These remain software proofs. The next step is
building and deploying the authoring Host release, then using the actual birth
and Crèche encounter and capturing its visible output.

### Actual installed browser admission, 2026-11-02

The authoring Host was updated to release `8751a6f23a6ef881b27adccfa911cb5bbd9c65e0448d9bcd89b36e811539d578` from source `f3e9b7f87707f28f968643c4c1c109ff20be97e7`, retaining Hamm Creek House and its Host identity. The next boundary failed: after the installed owner accepted a finite wake request, repeated Face refreshes reported `owner-mask-route-unavailable`. No authoring interaction or Seed production is claimed. Closing the browser also left the admission window reserved, including a run that clicked Leave immediately before closing; recovery required restarting the lulled owner service. Both observations require diagnosis, not bypassing authority checks. Threebee and the MCU firmware were unchanged. See `proof/evidence/creche/actual-browser-admission-20261102.json`; screenshots and browser logs are retained in the task output directory.

The subsequent source diagnosis found the remote route seal rejects any Face carrying a producer Plan or active Play, whereas the running Crèche exposes a genuine Plot Face with those identities. The local native Plot-owner custody work in #6470 is relevant, but explicitly does not deliver remote routing. The repair must validate the exact current producer rather than remove its provenance. One settled post-wake refusal was observed; a later retry screenshot caught an in-flight request and does not establish the outcome of every retry. The acceptance driver now waits for each refresh to settle; that revision has only been syntax checked so far.

The actual browser authoring journey continued through Source review and the final Check action. The original private invitation was neither edited nor published. Latimer's personal join remains unacknowledged; the user canceled that attempt because port forwarding was too cumbersome. The isolated product-proof browser is a separate admitted Host.

An intervening terminal biography capacity failure caused one service restart. Recovery retained the same Hamm Body and archived older evidence. A qualified source correction now rechecks terminal/recovery history room transactionally through existing archives after intervening membership events; it does not enlarge limits or erase history. This correction is integrated but not yet deployed. Exact Seed identities, Source, screenshots and non-claims are in `proof/evidence/creche/body-authored-threebee-seed-20261102.json`.

### Threebee boots a Body-made Seed and joins Hamm, 2026-11-02

The same installed Crèche manufactured a fresh Seed with an original, unmodified invitation. The verified native carrier installed that complete artifact on the physical Raspberry Pi 3 B+, preserving the previous installation inactive. Threebee then booted and completed ordinary signed admission into Hamm Creek House. The authoring owner and destination agree on the exact Body, Host, Boot and credential. Actual initialized offers contain `time/tick` and the authored eight-slot timer pool. No Plan or Play was created.

The carrier truthfully reports byte realization only; subsequent Boot and membership evidence establish the later lifecycle stages separately. SSH carried the Seed and signed admission messages as operator transport, not as a claimed execution Line. The first installer attempt stopped at a dangling systemd-unit symlink after state archival; the old state was restored before the corrected installer succeeded. Linux and the legacy HDMI renderer were preserved. That renderer still displays the old environmental Face: the Hamm graphical Seed and graph remain unfinished. Latimer joining was canceled by the user and is not a prerequisite for continuing. Exact public identities and scope are in `proof/evidence/creche/threebee-seed-admission-20261102.json`.

The integration head also passed the actual running Crèche remote-producer regression and all six selected-IMAGE custody tests. The latter cover finite managed backing, zero post-start allocation for the admitted tick path, refusal, cancellation and escaped result bytes; they are hosted CPU evidence, not MCU RAM or complete preparation-workspace proof. These source changes are not yet installed on the physical hosts.

## Corrected graphical Seed completes its finite first encounter — 2 November UTC

The checked native release at `7c8010111a` settles historical SDL preparation
notifications before issuing its live output token. Nine owning SDL cases,
three selected-Host suites and strict checks pass; post-token loss remains
terminal. The matching supported Make IMAGE and release agree on configuration,
profile, build and executable identity.

The next live attempt exposed a separate finite-state bug: all 16 retained
invitation records were expired, yet still occupied issuance capacity. The
Core repair at `8278b21c68` retires expired records during successful issuance,
preserves live consumed records, membership receipts and Part continuity keys,
and prevents identity reuse with a checked persisted sequence. Core tests,

### Signed installed-member return entrance, 2026-11-02

The next source implementation connects the retained private continuity signer
and crash-safe returned-credential storage to a finite pinned TLS member
encounter. This is an existing Part's return, not another invitation or another
birth. The owner checks its latest credential, makes one ten-second challenge,
verifies Core's signature proof against current biography, and durably commits
before replying. At most three failed proofs may use that challenge. Exact
completed replies can be recovered inside the same still-current challenge;
a withdrawal invalidates that cached response. No route timeout implies Host

The native startup selects this entrance for retained membership instead of
reading its consumed original Seed invitation. Its returned credential is

existing finite native Face route and acknowledge an actual rendered Show.

The generic installed CLI exposes `body member-route` for an explicitly

select that descriptor while the member is stopped, and `body member-leave`

retained Body/Part/Host, expiry, pin, attempt and timeout bounds. It grants no

part of ordinary authored Host construction; this operational entrance does
not complete that goal requirement.

### Owner-local current-member carrier custody

The owner now issues, completes, inspects and retires the existing current-member

challenge and one active session in lazily allocated bounded custody. Issuance

checking its proof; ten-second proof custody and both monotonic/wall session
deadlines prevent an expired issuance from becoming current authentication.

The active session retains its full member basis, not merely Host/Boot/generation.
Every inspection rechecks the latest exact credential and current advertisement.
A same-incarnation detach/reattach therefore invalidates old carrier custody and
allows a fresh session without waiting for the old deadline. Retirement drops
only the exact session; it never withdraws the still-running member. The existing
running-Body guard continues to refuse new issuance/completion, while authenticated
inspection/cleanup remain available. Control tokens are checked and cleared
through the existing entrances; replies contain public challenge/signature or
inspection facts, never the private owner key.

wrong-session/bad-proof consumption and withdrawal refusal; same-incarnation

### Renewable current-member reachability on retained TLS

The current-member entrance now retains the same authenticated TLS socket and
uses the existing participant framing and Core HostPresenceTable. Its protected
owner entrance starts sequence one, rechecks the exact current member and carrier
on every renewal, rejects repeated/out-of-bound sequences, and bounds each lease

missed lease refuses late renewal. If a worker dies before cleanup, its expired
started presence does not occupy the authentication slot until the longer carrier
deadline; fresh authentication can replace it without withdrawing the member.
Cleanup still names the exact session, so stale cleanup cannot retire a replacement.

The participant socket now accepts actual native or TLS carriers. TLS uses the
smaller of its initialized message bound and the existing protocol bound; buffers
and sends cannot silently enlarge that extent. TLS explicitly refuses the
browser-specific Line-evidence constructor. The independently accepted lease
replaces an absolute watchdog for that exact socket, including partial frames,
Ping/pong processing and blocked writes. Raw traffic never renews that deadline.

This is still a restricted reachability entrance: Face/Show/action frames refuse

### 3 November: preserve the member carrier's selected expiry

The intermittent pinned current-member carrier refusal had a concrete cause:
the route calculated a remaining duration before local actor scheduling, then
the owner added that duration to a later clock reading. Its signed challenge
could therefore exceed the independently selected route expiry, which the
client correctly refused. Integrated commit `2c6ae0619b` carries the absolute
selected expiry through authenticated local control and uses it directly.
The owner's one-hour cap remains enforced; its pending challenge deadline is
also capped by the session deadline. No retry margin or longer grant is added.

All eleven carrier module tests pass, including typed local-control round trips,
wrong-caller refusal, exact pending-slot retirement, presence expiry, receipt
scope and the new scheduling-delay regression. Invalid deadlines leave an
existing valid issuance intact. The real local pinned-TLS returning-member test
also passes with five-millisecond route-to-owner delays deliberately inserted;
it still checks lost committed replies, mutual current-member authentication
and continuing presentation. This replaces the earlier unexplained intermittent
expiry refusal with a deterministic tested bound.

This fix is published in the integration lane. The product carrier producer path
it changes is not yet present in dev, so its eventual focused extraction must
include this corrected absolute-expiry control field and matching callers.
No deployment or physical member acceptance follows from these tests. The
running owner is unchanged, project Pico udev rules remain absent, and the
known dev Integration remains active before its failed-job retry is permitted.

### HT16K33 protocol meaning moves into checked Source

The next execution work exposed a missing counterpart to the existing BME280
protocol Source: Green's display commands still lived in the diagnostic Rust
loop. [PR #6742](https://github.com/dancxjo/conduit/pull/6742) adds ordinary
HT16K33 initialization, transition, action, frame-request and write-outcome Plots
over the existing finite I2C Types. It introduces no target runtime or new Kind.

Initialization orders oscillator start, display disable, RAM clear, brightness
and display control, advancing only after each empty successful bus result.
Short, malformed and refused outcomes stop the sequence. Normal redraws use
one pointer-plus-16-octet transaction without a clear. Configuration, segment
mapping, selected controller possession and application observations remain
distinct responsibilities.

The isolated current-dev delivery commit `f4781b7105` passes both focused Source
tests and strict owning-test Clippy. The tests exercise brightness, blink and
enabled combinations at both address boundaries; every bus refusal at every
initialization phase; malformed acknowledgements; terminal-state stability;
invalid parameters; exact frame bytes; and zero allocation/reallocation during
prepared evaluation. This is CPU Source conformance. It does not establish
physical compatibility, whole-workset admission or a deployed ordinary Play.
The installed owner and Green firmware were not changed by this step.

### The display protocol executes through the ordinary kernel

The same PR now includes `ht16k33-feedback.conduit`. Its initialization entry
connects the generic terminal state cell and feedback zip to the selected I2C
Back; its redraw entry uses the existing finite merge for parameter and bus
failures. No target-side display coordinator was added. The acknowledgement has
an explicit record schema with the number of acknowledged transactions, keeping
its selected payload distinct from a primitive empty port. The merged refusal
Fore explicitly declares the same finite byte bound as its selected merge Back.
The planner's exact contracts and single-binding rules were preserved.

On isolated current-dev delivery head `09c9710f00`, five native-kernel tests and
the two prepared-expression tests pass. The kernel tests independently retain an
inert bus, publish its Ready Back, plan the actual Source, issue finite fixture
possession and execute the sole existing kernel. Five acknowledged writes close
initialization; a failed clear prevents enabling without a retry; redraw closes
after one transaction; a failed redraw yields one refusal; and an unsupported
address causes no bus effect. Formatting and diff hygiene pass. Existing native
Mask unit-build warnings remain outside this change.

These are CPU Source/kernel proofs, not physical or MCU workspace admission.
Green's diagnostic IO has not yet been replaced. The next physical step must
consume its actual retained controller through the same admitted operations,
with the full sampling/display workset prepared before changing local Play.

An isolated rerun exhausted the temporary incremental-cache quota. After the
compiler stopped, 133 inactive rustc incremental directories were removed,
recovering about 10.3 GiB in `/tmp`; the rerun passed with incremental compilation
disabled. Inactive native-release and ARMv6 build caches were byte-compared and
relocated to temporary storage, releasing about 5.5 GiB on the root filesystem.
Qualified images, credentials, records, installed software and source survived.

sequence selections allocated a private canonical result buffer, then copied it
into the enclosing evaluator's already-reserved output. The shared `no_std`

[PR #6743](https://github.com/dancxjo/conduit/pull/6743) carries only that shared

recovers. The indexed evaluator still separately retains its source collection.

Thirty-five tests across seven explicit targets pass on the isolated delivery
branch, together with strict owning-library/regression-test Clippy, formatting
and diff hygiene. The integration branch also checks the actual HT16K33 Source:

Declared capacities and dependencies were not increased. This is reusable
execution-memory progress under #6341, not complete workspace admission or a

### Resident fixed-scalar computation images (4 November 2026)

[PR #6745](https://github.com/dancxjo/conduit/pull/6745) separates the existing
fixed-scalar profile's authoring-time preparation from resident execution. The
construction helper emits a Rust instruction array, shared literal slices and
the exact canonical program digest. The destination borrows those instructions
and executes the same scalar tape loop into caller-owned output, with no Source
decoding or instruction/result heap allocation. Static lifetime does not prove
physical flash placement, and the digest does not grant execution authority.

The constructor checks Types, literal geometry, operand/destination bounds,
forward-only control flow, definite initialization on every branch and consumed
Move sources before execution. Its forward analysis uses a 129-byte state array;
the instruction limit remains 128 and execution uses the existing scratch
profiles. No native-law witness or arithmetic proof hint is silently erased.

On isolated delivery head `dd5948d52c`, nineteen tests and strict owning-library/
image-test Clippy pass, together with formatting and diff hygiene. Tests compile
generated modules both as a native executable and a `no_std` Rust library,
checking Source bytes/refusals, signed overflow, lazy branches, exact digests,
literal sharing and allocation-free resident construction/evaluation. This is a
fixed integer/Bool library component. Ordinary Make selection, installation as
a kernel Back, complete structured protocol images, MCU footprint and physical
workset admission remain unfinished. No MCU firmware was changed.

field, validator or storage ceiling was weakened.

### Explicit private Step-work reservations (4 November 2026)

Installation review found a real missing connection: ordinary lowering supplied

A Host must offer finite capacity and a Back must require it through the existing
resource contract. Ordinary planning refuses absent or insufficient pools and
seals the selected amount and pool identity. Lowering adds only those exact
bindings to the existing IO grant, retains their resource bindings and refuses
malformed or unrepresentable grants. No new serialized fields, scheduler,
transport or environmental policy were introduced. Nodes without the selected
resource retain their prior fuel. Cooperative units are not CPU time or hostile
code containment.

while retaining the Back and can subsequently surrender the same Back for

configuration rather than retaining a second decoded program in target RAM.

final generated-source ceiling includes the metadata and initialized wrapper.

### Attribute exact command bytes before preparation (4 November 2026)

The Body layer now provides an issued, one-use command challenge. It borrows

pattern with a distinct transcript domain; admission-receipt signatures cannot
be reused as command proofs.

issuance remains Root's obligation, and reconstructing a slot with an old nonce
is not replay protection. Successful attribution lends the same payload bytes;
it grants no membership, Plan approval, execution, protected Line or Host Call
possession. Root must still apply its policy and actual finite admission.
Fresh preparation and retained-reservation lifecycle control require different
independently selected scope; this helper cannot choose between them.

`4e0d65a99d` first hit the native release guard: 67,392,920 bytes exceeds the
unchanged 64 MiB artifact limit by 284,056 bytes. A package-local `opt-level =
"s"` policy reduced loadable code but initially left symbol tables in the file.
The final explicit `strip = "symbols"` override, matching the existing release
policy, seals Source `5b26290623` at **64,260,288 bytes**, with **2,848,576 bytes**
headroom. Execution/provider dependencies retain normal release optimization.
The exact sealed file hash and reviewed construction receipt were independently
verified; a cached repeat of the Make entrance returned zero. This is a native
artifact code-generation change, not a claim about MCU RAM.

Make produced Green's public application and reviewed artifact from that same
clean Source. The linked firmware contains **983,308 text bytes** and **116,920
static RAM bytes**, including the unchanged 49,152-byte heap arena. Compared
with installed Source `b47cdd80f9`, text drops by **274,888 bytes**, while static
RAM grows by 40 bytes. These are linked extents; stack and simultaneous Root
workspace high-water are not measured by them. The public UF2 has 3,842 blocks.
Private retained-Part construction produces an identical application plus a
separate sixteen-block Body bootstrap; it creates no new invitation or Part.

The authoring Host's declarative Crèche initialization now pins verified private
copies of this fresh reviewed MCU image, artifact and physical Host Source. A
second cached checked-native release binds that configuration. Before install,
the service was stopped and all sixty regular state files were independently
hashed and checked against a compressed archive; installed releases remain in
place, and the verified previous release remains available for rollback.
Ordinary stopped-service configuration and verified installation, followed by
systemd reload/start, run the qualified new executable. Readback proves the same
persistent Host, Hamm Creek House and all sixteen Part identities/admission
states, on a new owner Boot with zero service restarts.

The carrier key is intentionally in-memory and scoped to that owner Boot. An
initial operational assertion that it would remain unchanged was wrong; source
inspection confirmed fresh CSPRNG issuance. The private MCU Seed instead pins
the actual qualified new peer through protected owner control. Green's member
secret/verifying key and latest previously accepted credential remain unchanged.
The old attachment was already absent after owner recovery; an explicit leave
refused as non-current, so no additional leave or new Part was created.

The first BOOTSEL command incorrectly used `make pico-local bootsel`. That alias
started its whole local workflow rather than honoring the explicit subcommand.
The parent and remaining compiler were stopped before any flash; the old device
build remained present. This tool defect is [#6753](https://github.com/dancxjo/conduit/issues/6753).
The existing exact `make pico bootsel` entrance acknowledged the expected old
build and selected the correct USB 1-2 device. The installed narrow headless
mount helper carried bootstrap and application separately. A preliminary
geometry assertion used the wrong bootstrap location and refused before any
write; final verification derives all ranges from the canonical flash layout.
Bootstrap occupies `0x101ff000..0x10200000`; application pages stop before the
retained network sectors. The bootstrap and application hashes, native IMAGE,
configuration, original member key and pins were checked before transfer.
No raw flash readback is claimed.

Green returned over USB and saved Wi-Fi with independently predicted build
`646382b8...787cd9e1`. All five resident Backs initialized; local diagnostic
measurement and display resumed without reported faults. The new ordinary
`body return-usb` performed actual signed publication, full Linux-side image

image references and 1,361 bytes**. The MCU accepted raw digest
`a41dc368...1846aa24`; it matches the saved signed receipt. Owner readback shows

`rp2040/boot/83f03c633138ff7fc3c0ff3f00e0c139`, in the same Body with sixteen
Parts. No capability catalog was decoded on the MCU. Its instrumented heap
peak through this exchange is **4,356 bytes**, not a complete stack/Root peak.

After the USB client closed, samples advanced from four to twelve and
acknowledged diagnostic redraws from 54 to 175, with no sensor/display error

### 4 November: exclusive retained inventory without async copies

The firmware's initialized Back inventory previously escaped as a permanent
shared static reference. That prevented Root from mutably borrowing the same
retained implementations for exact binding. It now remains in one static
allocation and is acquired through a non-clonable exclusive lease. Competing
acquisition reports Busy; failed initialization and absent initialization have
distinct refusal states. Dropping the lease returns the pointer, including when
an async borrower is cancelled. Initialization status remains observable during
a lease but does not claim provider availability or an active reservation.
Membership publication borrows through that same lease; no wire format changes.

The regression test compiles the actual firmware custody module with Green's
checked five-Back library, executes it natively and compiles its no_std source.
It proves exclusive acquisition, mutable access, pointer identity after return,
no scoped allocations during initialization/acquisition/return/cancellation, and
return after cancellation of a polled pending future. On this 64-bit CPU the
retained inventory is 21,632 bytes, its lease is eight bytes, and the tested
pending future is sixteen bytes. These are CPU layout measurements, not MCU
stack or heap peaks. The standalone Pico firmware also passes a locked release
ARM target check. Strict RP2040 Make Clippy passes with all targets and warnings
denied. Raw outputs remain in ignored target/resident-custody-20261104/.

This is Rust ownership custody only. It does not admit work, reserve resources,
quiesce the diagnostic I2C owner, authorize effects or prove same-Boot Play
replacement. No device or live service was changed. The next execution boundary
still requires actual Root resource admission and protected equipment handover;
#6341's physical workspace gate remains open. The ARM check uses the retained
reviewed IMAGE for compilation only and is not a newly fabricated Seed.

A completed native regression-test executable (about 269 MB) was removed after
its process ended; source, logs, deployed software, credentials, history and
recovery artifacts were preserved. Subsequent ARM checking consumed part of
that headroom; root has about 529 MiB available, so further large builds require
another verified cache inventory and reclamation first.


### 4 November: local Fore binding for the shared I2C operation

Tracing the next physical handover found an existing shared-mechanism gap:
ReadyI2cBase accepted compact native assignments but had no explicit local Fore
path, unlike the existing TM1637 provider. It now exposes check_assigned_fore
and bind_assigned_fore_retained through the same exact assigned-operation
checker. Both paths retain the existing local identity, provider/resource
freshness, opaque possession and codec checks before controller transfer.
They introduce no new decoder, kernel, authority or network Line.

All fourteen I2C unit tests pass. The assignment fixture now exercises both
contiguous and borrowed-record storage, with and without real typed request and
result Fore ports. The Fore path rejects absent, incomplete and altered ABI pins;
the plain path continues to reject the Fore schema. Stale Boot, stale resource
generation and narrowed-invalid parameter claims preserve the provider. A
successful bind takes it once, retains no received assignment borrow, and keeps
address refusal, replay rejection and revocation behavior. The fixtures use an
inert CPU provider, not physical traffic or ordinary Body execution.

The two 4096-byte Fore queues exceed the old fixture's 8192-byte runtime budget;
its 8280-byte projection is refused at that budget. The successful two-queue
fixture explicitly admits 16384 bytes. The original no-Fore fixture retains its
8192-byte bound. No firmware capacity or grant was enlarged. Strict all-feature,
all-target I2C Clippy and the standalone locked Pico release ARM check pass.
This compile check is not a new Seed or physical memory-peak measurement.

Green remains on its prior reviewed build: live readback reports 78 samples in
78 attempts and 1170 acknowledged diagnostic frames with no sensor/display
fault and an installed membership receipt. No device/service was changed.
Actual admitted equipment handover, ordinary sensing/display Plots and #6378's
physical output acceptance remain open, alongside #6341's workspace gate.
Raw logs and status are ignored under target/i2c-assigned-fore-20261104/.
Before building, verified inactive test executables in the owned cache were
removed, reclaiming 745233640 bytes while preserving active products, source,
private state, rollback artifacts and evidence.

all existing refusal checks and exact release behavior remain in force. No new

A scoped native allocation regression first failed on the old implementation.
For the same two-placement batch and one prior reservation, adding 128 unrelated
valid resource pools raised preparation from 25 allocations / 2146-byte peak to
281 allocations / 52596-byte peak. After the change both inventories take seven
allocations, zero reallocations and a 478-byte measured peak, requesting 574
bytes in total. Host construction, requests and existing reservations are made
outside the measured scope; these numbers exclude stack and their retained
storage. The regression requires identical allocation counts and byte costs as
unrelated inventory grows. It proves removal of the preparation copy, not an
allocation-free owner or a physical MCU workspace peak.

All seven resource-admission integration tests and six related content and
collection tests pass. Strict core Clippy passes for all targets; the no-default-
features release core check passes on thumbv6m-none-eabi. Existing late-refusal
coverage verifies unchanged old reservations and compute assignments after a
batch fails. Raw before/after, lint and target logs remain ignored under target/.
No firmware or installed service was changed. #6341's physical gate remains
open; actual declared work capacity, finite command reception, equipment
possession and admitted sensing/display execution still need integration.

### 4 November: actual compiler preparation cost, separated from provider payload

The production-Host harness now measures construction of the real reviewed text
catalogue, immutable Source/check context, canonical compiler/cache, Native schema
and prepared provider together. CPU x86_64 debug/System-allocator observations:

| Source | Source bytes | Scoped heap peak | Retained provider payload | Allocations | Reallocations | Requested bytes |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Repository Hello | 179 | 2,767,780 | 8,997 | 105,875 | 4,683 | 17,028,789 |
| Invalid Unicode Source | 50 | 2,763,616 | 8,568 | 104,375 | 4,555 | 16,709,198 |

Raw repeatable output is `target/source-check-complete-preparation-cost.log`;
`cargo +stable test -p conduit-body-make --test source_check_call
complete_preparation_costs --locked -- --nocapture` repeats it. This scoped heap
observation excludes stack, allocator overhead, existing process state and other
threads, and does not include later Host installation/authoring/Plan construction.
It is not RSS, MCU use, a worst-case bound or enforced preparation containment.
The compiler/catalogue owners are dropped before the returned provider is read;
the subsequent malformed-request refusal still measures zero allocations.
Strict all-target Body Make Clippy and formatting pass.

The measured peak is over 300 times the retained provider payload. Admitting only
that payload cannot establish the preparation contract. The existing Base
preparation ceiling is explicitly cooperative accounting; using its token alone
would not change this conclusion. Existing isolated Linux providers do enforce
address-space/CPU limits, but transferring this checker's private catalogue would
need exact retained compiler-context custody, not reconstruction from descriptive
revision strings or an ambient default catalogue. This is the next S2 service
boundary to resolve, with no MCU compiler, invented grant or duplicate transport.

### 4 November: parent-side bounded compiler preparation and retirement

The Linux Source preparation parent now uses the existing hosted-process polling

a Stop observed before publication discards even completed output. The result
is descriptive bytes, not a Native admission witness or executable candidate.

### Identify immutable Source without constructing a parser (5 November 2026)

Preparing a Source snapshot previously parsed its whole document solely to ask
for its authored-byte identity. `canonical_source_document_id` now streams the
existing `canonical-source:` byte domain directly into SHA-256 and allocates
only the 64-byte returned identity. SyntaxDocument delegates to it; immutable
Source-check snapshot construction uses it without a temporary lexer, token
vector or AST. This changes no identity domain, normalization, grammar,
checking result, Source grant or executable owner.

Golden/legacy-domain tests include unfinished Source, UTF-8, BOM, CRLF and
byte-significant whitespace. Identity allocation is exactly one 64-byte result
for empty, 1 KiB and 1 MiB inputs. In the allocation-probed 1 MiB token-heavy
fixture, the former parse-for-identity step peaked at 10,616,832 bytes across
131,078 allocations and 17 reallocations. The new complete immutable snapshot
constructor uses 1,048,737 newly allocated bytes in four allocations: the Source
bytes plus 161 bytes of measured metadata. Context/catalogues already have their
separate owner; these are CPU allocator observations, not MCU RAM or whole
compiler/RSS measurements.

UTF-8 and IO errors refuse distinctly without enlarging storage or hiding a

checks its selected executable and actual child context. No request label can
cause another resource to be opened. Filesystem publication/atomicity and IO

### 2026-11-05: Capped presence receipts preserve their original deadline

Source inspection explains the end-of-window refusal observed on Threebee: the
current-member session is explicitly sixty seconds, and the owner may return an
unchanged expiry as its final leases are capped. The client previously required
each expiry to increase and treated a valid capped receipt as malformed.

The client now advances the exact receipt sequence for equal capped expiries
without extending its existing local deadline. Later owner-clock expiry advances
can extend that deadline only by the corresponding delta, further bounded by
the existing two-second receipt bound and overall session deadline. Validation,
late-arrival and overflow refusals precede any timing-state update. This changes
neither the wire protocol nor grants, frame budgets, session authorization or
initial receipt timing assumptions; it provides no new clock-synchronization or
continuing-session authority.

receipts and expiration without mutation. The existing actual pinned-TLS
signed-return/lost-reply regression also passes. Strict product binary/tests
Clippy passes. This checkpoint is local source proof and has not replaced the
physical Threebee release. Its previous native Show remains historical.

The Body-made Seed above was prepared with ordinary `make host prepare-seed
--retained-member`. The owner pin was read through the authenticated existing
local member-carrier control request and checked against the live Host/Boot/
generation. Its proof key changes with the owner Boot; the previous Boot's pin
was not reused. The first prepared input contained an older member credential;
this was caught before attempting return and replaced from the owner's latest
committed receipt, preserving the original member secret and Part.

UF2 page addresses were independently checked before submission: application
payloads remain below the bootstrap sector, and all bootstrap payloads lie
within 0x101ff000..0x10200000. The application hash matches the previously
qualified enabled image. Exact build-bound BOOTSEL acknowledgements selected
Green's runtime UID e6639c758f40702c; ROM serial E0C9125B0D9B was checked before
the narrowly authorized headless mount carrier submitted the files. No full
flash erase, full-flash backup/readback, or unchanged-journal-byte proof is
claimed. Updating the bootstrap required a second bounded transfer after the
stale input was identified. The owner had already withdrawn the old attachment;
ordinary member-leave refused without changing it.

Ordinary installed `body return-usb` then committed the signed existing-member
return and delivered its saved receipt. Green reports that exact receipt digest
`74c749e79b08e9eccf5bb778aea4813bf27bb474af0b7a9c0a8b78e34ab58791`
and body_member true. The owner's current Part agrees on Boot
`rp2040/boot/c78e1c9ccce3071e611cc31ce31cc380`. Membership remains 16 Parts and
15 continuity keys. Actual firmware build is
`40f52a0256670311150fb509aa8e12ce7c66041022586df61fa06d27b94a9c7e`;
configuration matches the enabled 9c159217... identity above. Initial physical
status records one sample, ten acknowledged frames, five initialized resident
Backs, 19,016 backing bytes, 4,924 allocator peak bytes and no sensor/display
fault. These are component measurements, not total RAM or stack high-water.

This closes the previously unflashed enabled-image gap. It does not prove a
prepared command exchange, native resource installation/Start, admitted local
sampling/display Plays, or restart-persistent advancement of the new credential.
Those remain next; diagnostic sensing/display is still explicitly distinct.
Private bootstrap, current input and signed return receipt remain outside Git;
raw physical status is retained under target/green-enabled-seed-20261105.


### Physical preparation challenge and deadline recovery

On the enabled, admitted Green Boot above, the existing bounded CDC framing
carried `conduit.body/prepare-fragments@1`. The actual receiver returned a
Host-command challenge for Hamm Creek House, its observed Host/Boot and enabled
configuration, the pinned current owner Host/Boot/key, contract
`conduit.body/prepared-workset@1`, maximum payload 4,096 bytes and exactly
10,000 milliseconds of receiver-monotonic validity. Those fields were checked
against independently retained live owner/member facts before proceeding.
No command was signed or submitted.

After the original deadline, a second physical request obtained a distinct
nonce on the same Boot with issuance later than the first expiry. Thus an
abandoned challenge does not permanently occupy the input workspace. Diagnostic
IO continued to seven samples and 100 acknowledged frames with membership true
and no sensor/display errors. This is physical challenge/deadline recovery,
not proof of signature acceptance, resource preparation or retained payload.
Public challenge/status captures are under target/green-enabled-seed-20261105.

Tracing the next real handoff confirms that `generate_prepared_body_fragment`
is the existing host-side factory: it consumes `PreparedBodyFragment` rich
containment evidence and generates the canonical native projection. Neither
the installed Linux owner nor its transport currently calls that factory or
`encode_body_workset_document`. Their current callers are scoped CPU tests.
The installed authoring path must next derive the complete exact local workset
from its real Body Plan/Play, approve it and send it through the pinned current
Host-command exchange. Signing a fixture or arbitrary wire bytes would bypass
that missing integration and is deliberately not presented as progress toward
an admitted Body Play. Receiver-local reservation/installation/Start and physical
bus handover remain separately required under #6770/#6341.


### Authoring-side complete workset derivation

`PreparedBodyFragment::local_fragments` now borrows every fragment assigned to
that Host from the same validated Body Plan/Play, including selected Mask
chains. Each sibling retains the original unambiguous-containment law: an
otherwise valid anchor cannot produce a witness for a sibling repeated in two
containing plans. Iteration, including that refusal, allocates nothing.

`generate_prepared_body_workset` derives that entire set instead of accepting a
caller-selected fragment list. It preflights the existing finite count and
independently supplied current Host/Boot/generation before calling an
implementation resolver. Each fragment then goes through the existing exact
canonical projection factory; the unchanged complete-set encoder verifies the
aggregate document and byte ceiling. The returned immutable document has no
public constructor or deserializer. It is still no signature, execution
permission, resource reservation or evidence of remote capability possession.

The emitted-code resident-offer test passes for both two ordinary Plots and a
Plot plus selected Mask chain. Either anchor produces identical complete bytes.
Foreign Host, Boot and generation refuse before the resolver, zero capacity
refuses before resolution, and an undersized aggregate byte ceiling refuses.
A separate ambiguous-sibling case retains one valid anchor and reports both
ambiguous occurrences with zero scoped allocations. Existing Root validation,
reservation, failure and retirement cases continue to pass within that same
owning test. The two emitted-code top-level tests pass; strict Body and embedded
build library/test Clippy passes, and authenticated-admission Body checks with
no default features for thumbv6m-none-eabi. Logs are retained under
/tmp/conduit-derived-workset-final2-{test,clippy}.log and
/tmp/conduit-derived-workset-thumb.log.

This is reusable authoring preparation, not installed owner delivery. The live
owner still needs to invoke this factory with its real current Body Plan/Play,
approve the exact resulting bytes and use the existing attributed exchange.
The MCU firmware and its finite grants were not changed for this step.


### Owner proposal custody reaches the workset generator

The installed owner now has `prepare_remote_body_workset`, which invokes the
complete-workset generator using its own retained lifecycle proposal. It first
requires its original idle Host, an independently current admitted member offer,
and an unstarted checked proposal. It chooses the containing fragment from that
proposal, derives rich containment evidence, and returns the canonical document
with its prospective Body Play identity. No approval bytes or replacement Plan
are accepted from a caller. The selected implementation resolver remains an
explicit input; an unavailable registry refuses instead of substituting a Back.

The Host's existing prospective Body Play identity calculation is exposed as
`prospective_body_play` and is shared by ordinary execution. It validates the
same Wake/Plan and finite sequence without consuming it or issuing a grant.
The focused existing complete-workset execution test now checks that repeated
inspection consumes nothing and that the actual successful kernel report uses
exactly that identity. This small three-file prerequisite is isolated on current
dev in PR #6784, head bd156d9f93. The owner integration remains on the delivery
branch; exact isolated-branch hosted qualification is still pending.

A real owner/current-member planning fixture reaches the implementation resolver
with the retained Plan, Source and prospective Play, then honestly refuses its
missing native registry. Missing proposal, unobserved generation and withdrawn
member refuse before resolution. Resolver refusal preserves the biography,
proposal, sequence and absence of execution. This is CPU owner integration and
refusal evidence, not successful native projection/delivery. Both focused tests,
product binary checking and strict product binary Clippy pass on the combined
delivery checkout. Logs remain /tmp/conduit-owner-workset-{check,test,clippy}.log
and /tmp/conduit-owner-prospective-play-test.log. The evidence-retention checker
passes when called directly; the cached xtask does not expose the documented
`ci pipeline` entrance, so that unsupported command is not counted as proof.

The new owner method is not yet dispatched by a Face/control operation. Current
provider implementation resolution, exact command approval/signing, bounded USB
carriage, receiver installation/Start and physical bus handover remain required.
The live owner release and Green firmware were not changed in this step. Green
continued through 44 samples and 654 acknowledged frames, membership true and
sensor/display errors false.

### Current resident implementation resolution

The owner now has a concrete resident resolver entry alongside the earlier
preparation hook. `prepare_remote_resident_workset` calls the existing complete
workset generator and resolves every selected fragment with
`bind_current_resident_expressions`. That function requires a verified sealed
Plan, matching independently observed Host/Boot/generation, exactly one current
offer for each selected capability, and exactly one prepared image matching the
entire offer. The existing binder still checks Source, artifact, program,
contract, work requirement and exact placement. No Source/authority guard was
removed, and descriptive offers are not treated as local capability possession.

Prepared images are borrowed from the authoring side; no second persistent offer
catalog or firmware runtime was introduced. A missing image, changed offer or
ambiguous selection refuses rather than selecting the first candidate. This is
currently the resident-expression profile: it does not relabel an arbitrary
hosted Face provider or bus effect as a resident implementation.

The emitted-code resident-offer suite now resolves against the original current
advertisement before producing every delegated document. Its ordinary Plot and
Plot-plus-Mask cases continue through existing Root validation/reservation and
typed kernel tests. New checks reject foreign Host/Boot/generation, withdrawn or
duplicate offers, altered capacity, missing images and duplicate matching
images. A later independently current bind succeeds without consuming the
prepared image. Both top-level emitted-code tests pass. The owner test also
exercises the concrete missing-registry refusal and verifies unchanged lifecycle
proposal and sequence. Strict embedded-build and product binary Clippy passes.
Logs are /tmp/conduit-current-resident-{resolution-test,resolution-clippy,
owner-test,owner-clippy}.log.

This is CPU implementation resolution, not successful live owner delivery. The

### Bounded CDC carriage for an independently approved command

`PicoSpawnSocket::exchange_preparation` now uses the existing stream framing and
bounded-document chunk format for the preparation request, original challenge,
owner approval callback, exact bytes, signature and final report. One original
sender deadline covers all stages, including approval work. A shorter receiver
lifetime can only shorten that deadline; clock epochs are never compared.
The complete payload remains bounded by the existing 4,096-byte receiver frame
ceiling, chunks use 512-byte scratch and the unchanged 64-chunk limit. No new
transport, retry scheduler, Source checker or execution engine was introduced.

A receiver already reporting retained input returns Occupied without invoking
the approval callback. A final input report must match the exact submitted
SHA-256 and explicitly report RAM custody, resources released and Start not
installed. The result is named a reported input, not a signed preparation,
admission or Play receipt. Failures after submission begins, wrong/missing
reports, and replies after the original deadline return OutcomeUnknown; they
never imply rollback, safe replacement, resource release or execution.

Six deterministic courier tests pass. They reassemble exact chunks with the
existing borrowed receiver assembler, preserve the signature frame, check
monotonically shrinking timeouts, and cover occupied input, finite challenge
and payload bounds, refused/slow approval, zero deadline, shorter receiver
lifetime, first-chunk failure and lost/wrong/late final reports. These tests use
synthetic opaque bytes and claim only courier protocol behavior. Strict std
library/test Clippy passes. Logs remain under
/tmp/conduit-preparation-courier-{verified-tests,final-clippy}.log.

Before the first physical retained-input submission, recovery needs one concrete
receiver correction: its occupied-input branch reads control into 64 bytes,
while the existing build-bound BOOTSEL request is 25 prefix bytes plus the
64-byte build ID (89 bytes). The short query can succeed but the reboot request
cannot fit that buffer. This was found by inspecting the actual Link copy and
BOOTSEL handler, not by changing a device. Preserve the original document while
providing enough bounded maintenance-frame scratch; verify the ingress frame
ceiling before qualifying another image. Do not knowingly seal the live input
and strand the existing software recovery route.

and formatting pass. Logs remain /tmp/conduit-retained-recovery-final-test.log
and /tmp/conduit-retained-recovery-clippy.log.

offer, readiness claim or authority.

lease implements the existing I2C provider boundary, so it can be consumed by the

preparation leaves them alone; dropped/cancelled leases return the same controller.
Revocation preserves quarantine and blocks another native lease, with no hidden
reset/retry. After native use, diagnostics reinitialize before measuring again.
This is target hardware custody, not another Body scheduler or application relay.

bytes and borrow those immutable bytes; each output reserves only its derived
maximum. Transport limits, result semantics, authority and admitted grants are
unchanged. The generic leaf-sequence encoder exposes retained heap capacity so

and retains its refusal behavior. Selection allocates nothing.

### 5 November 2026 — Native Root dispatch replaces handwritten proof dispatch

`BoundNativeKernel::dispatch_one` now services at most one pending I2C Host
Call through the already bound local possession and the ordinary kernel
completion interface. It does not step the scheduler, grant Start, receive
transport input, retry a request or allocate another buffer. Physical bus
outcomes retain the existing typed result contract. Adapter refusal completes
the pending request with a standard failed/denied outcome and returns its typed
cause to Root; a rejected completion releases any unqueued output value.

The actual native Root CPU proof uses this production method instead of its
handwritten dispatch sequence. A valid typed request produces exactly one
provider effect and exact typed egress; another poll is idle. A malformed
second request produces a typed refusal and actual `BackFailed` kernel result,
without another provider effect or redispatch. Cancellation and terminal
retirement still drain the kernel, and binding/execution/retirement remain
allocation-free. All 14 parser-free I2C library tests pass. Raw proof is retained

### 5 November 2026 — Separate explicit execution intent in checked Host Source

Host construction now accepts the optional singleton
`execution = {authorizer: "seed-owner", maximum_active_worksets: 1}` separately
from `preparation`. The checker requires selected finite preparation and a
positive workset ceiling within the Host's existing active-instance limit.
Unknown/missing/duplicate fields or authorizers refuse. Canonical Source,
configuration identity and IMAGE correspondence retain the selection; selecting
preparation alone emits no execution policy. Generated Rust carries the exact
typed optional selection without adding a runtime parser. This declaration is
construction intent, not local capability possession or a resource grant.

The ordinary Host-configuration Face exposes execution separately, preserves it
through Check/Rename and refuses target replacement before dropping delegation.
Browser and terminal recipe constructors retain explicit absence or preserve the
existing selection as appropriate. The branch-local RP2040 build carries it as

### 2026-11-06 — Remove duplicate preparation from the delivery future

Receiver inspection exposed a real duplication: `serve_preparation` prepared a
resident-only workset, provisionally reserved and released it, and only then
retained its original command. The persistent worker subsequently prepared and
admitted the same work again. Besides duplicate work, the delivery future thus
owned profile-specific setup and rejected mixed input before its actual worker
could consider it.

The carrier now authenticates bounded bytes, checks the installed receive/
preparation policy under current independent facts, and seals the original
attribution in the existing input buffer. Profile decoding, complete validation,
prepared storage, native possession and admission remain the persistent worker's
responsibility. No parser, reservation or executable kernel is created in the
carrier future. The shared existing workset policy check is publicly accessible
for this read-only boundary; it neither decodes nor approves the payload.

The input report and Linux courier now agree on `resources: not-admitted` and
`start: not-started`. A receipt proves RAM custody of the reported bytes, never
valid assignment, admission, Start or durable recording. An authenticated opaque
or malformed assignment may be retained; it still cannot execute without the
worker's complete validation. Lost, cancelled or timed-out acknowledgments retain
sealed input, while authentication/receive failures leave no retained command.
The original deadline is never renewed. Existing admission/Back-custody gates
still prevent unsafe expiry reclamation and another attempt while occupied.

The owning test removed its synthetic prepare/reserve implementation entirely.
Actual handler tests cover zero-allocation receive/custody, invalid signatures,
digests, interruption, cancellation, deadline, fresh-nonce replay refusal,
occupied storage, expiry and build-bound recovery. This change removes 134 net
lines from receiver/courier/test code and one whole duplicate preparation pass;
no physical peak reduction or resolution of #6341's historical panic is claimed.
