### 6 November 2026 — physical controller custody limits the combined native workset

A read-only audit of Green's actual profile and native preparation distinguishes
a resource contract from a binder-size limitation. The profile selects one I2C1
controller (GP6/GP7) with BME280 address 118 and HT16K33 address 112. Its address
range does not imply two independently ownable controllers. The I2C offer charges
one attachment unit per placement; projection publishes capacity one. The actual
RP2040 owner lends one exclusive controller lease to one native Host Call. Simply
increasing mixed-preparation node counts or advertising capacity two would break
that custody correspondence. No such change was made.

An authored size-one shared pool bounds members, not controller consumers. The
planner reserves units times member capacity after ordinary placements; shared
resource IDs do not deduplicate separate placement demand. Current fragment-set
and executable-region lowering explicitly refuse shared pools, and the pool
controller neither invokes providers nor supplies protocol routing. Pool syntax
therefore cannot admit the presently unsupported combined native workset.

Relevant sources: `mechanisms/io/i2c-transaction/src/installation/{offer,projection,assigned}.rs`,
`targets/rp2040/firmware/host/src/{i2c_owner,i2c_installation,mixed_preparation}.rs`,
`architecture/planner/src/canonical/shared_pools.rs`, and
`architecture/plan-lowering/src/{fragment_set,shared_pool_runtime}.rs`.
The next static-composition investigation is whether ordinary authored request/
result routing can serve both protocols through one selected I2C placement. It
still needs complete resident lowering, routing, bounded workset admission and
physical execution; this audit proves none of those. Dynamic pool execution is
not a prerequisite unless that composition actually requires dynamic members.

Issue ownership stays precise: #4833 owns physical primitives versus authored
protocols; #6341 owns preparation/workspace admission. #6340 concerns retained
reservation authority across offer-generation changes, not a general permission
to share an exclusive attachment. No new memory epic or arbitration framework.

The current-dev x86_64 journey failure was inspected at Integration 42211111830,
job 127885501263: a Source-editor observation differed from the authorized handoff.
Existing PR #6817 owns the bounded checkpoint-observation repair; it retains
exact assertions and was still running target checks. Do not duplicate that work
or infer whole-product acceptance from a passing admission lane. #6815 remains
unmerged at e4f1a439ba with its admission job confirmed active. Downloaded CI
evidence is retained under ignored `target/dev-334600a4-journey-diagnostics`.

Maintenance removed the inactive Source-check review target cache and regenerated
Body-make package artifacts (about 6.7 GiB total); retained source, proof logs,
firmware, credentials and live services remain. Root headroom reached 7.8 GiB;
/tmp reached 15 GiB. No device/service was redeployed in this checkpoint.

### 6 November 2026 — static I2C routing experiment exposes terminal mismatch

An ordinary Source experiment tagged two caller streams, merged their requests
through one `machine/i2c/transact` placement, paired each FIFO context/result
through `flow/zip/finite`, and selected the tagged result back to its caller.
The actual ordinary planner accepted exactly one native I2C placement against
the unchanged capacity-one attachment. Preparation issued no bus effects.

CPU kernel transcript tests then disproved this topology's required terminal
behavior. With both inputs closed after admitting their finite requests, one
case completed with only the first caller's result. A case with only one caller
remained Active after 512 bounded steps, despite issuing its real inert-provider
transaction. Tests require both exact success bytes and a separately routed
not-acknowledged result; neither check was relaxed. Early test attempts corrected
a Rust sequence-value shape name and the Source record/variant declaration
syntax; the final failures concern actual kernel lifetime, not parsing.

Inspection of `architecture/composite/src/flow_zip.rs` explains why ordinary
finite zip is unsuitable as a request/reply drain: normal close flushes only
an already formed pair and can discard unmatched retained/queued values. Its
existing documented semantics were not changed. `flow/zip/feedback` has a
different state-return obligation and is not an interchangeable spelling.
This is evidence of a missing composition/lifetime contract, not proof that
physical controller sharing is impossible and not permission to lose replies.

The experiment is excluded from production Source and tests. Exact Source,
CPU test and failed transcript are retained in ignored
`target/i2c-two-client-experiment-20261106`; a replay patch is retained there too.
No runtime service, new Kind, inflated capacity or firmware deployment was
introduced. The next static-composition step requires an explicit bounded
request/reply correlation and drain contract, or a proven existing operation
with that lifetime. #4833 owns this protocol-composition dependency; #6341
continues to own memory admission. The earlier installed Source-action gap
remains an independent lifecycle blocker.

### 6 November 2026 — exact finite pairing repairs the one-controller composition

The experimentally missing close law is tracked by #6818, separate from MCU
workspace admission. `flow/zip/exact` is now an explicit semantic Kind with a
no_std prepared Back. It reuses the existing tuple encoder and fixed buffers,
retains one pending value per side across independent input closes, and refuses
unequal stream lengths rather than discarding a context or inventing a reply.
Finite and feedback zip identities and behavior are preserved.

The ordinary kernel caught an initial invalid terminal implementation that
passed isolated stepping: exposed terminal truth must be consumed according to
the selected profile even while flushing a formed pair. The final Back and
Kind use the existing bounded FlushThenPropagateWhenAllClose contract, consume
close alongside an admitted final pair, and wait under output pressure before
that phase. No scheduler law or resource grant was weakened or enlarged.

The original static I2C Source now selects exact zip explicitly. Its actual
Plan contains precisely one I2C placement against the unchanged exclusive
capacity-one owner. CPU Plan/Play tests pass for both client admission orders,
exact successful read bytes, a separately routed not-acknowledged result, and
an empty caller while the other continues. The full I2C factory group passes
20 cases, including the existing HT16K33 initialization/redraw and BME280
state/join execution. These are inert-provider CPU transcripts, not hardware.

The Back/semantic groups pass 21 zip regressions, including early closure from
either side, delayed counterparts, exact canonical output, pressure, empty
inputs, unequal lengths, abnormal termination and cancellation. The prepared
early-close/pressure execution probe records zero allocations. Strict composite
and semantic-catalog library/test Clippy and workspace formatting pass.
Logs are retained under ignored `target/exact-fifo-pair-20261106`.

The ConduitOS Source package selects the new exact operation through explicit
ExactZip specialization and an exact retained offer. This is ordinary typed
composition, not a new bus arbiter, transport or application coordinator. It
does not yet expose both real device protocol callers on RP2040, enlarge the
two-node mixed binder, prove complete-workset memory fit, or deploy a device.
The new semantic/Back slice is being qualified separately on current dev;
installed Source action selection and the full lifecycle journey remain open.

A bounded read-only audit of the actual owner, Source installation and Fore

selection. Therefore neither publishing a Source provider nor adding a transient
Plan entry can lawfully supply the missing execution.

Correct next integration sequence, within existing #6449/#6447 ownership:

   the Crèche Plot. S4 must check actual Source with admitted preparation; it
   cannot make an already installed S2 Play its only bootstrap authority.

   provide an adapter or Source possession.

   Source selection, validate current Body/revision, mint the original
   operation-specific Host/Boot grant, and initialize the selected compiler
   under its existing staged memory custody. Do not create a second Host inside
   an owner action.
4. Execute the now-resident Source-check Plot through ordinary Plan/Play. A typed
   report remains non-authoritative and does not adopt a candidate or Wake it.

The newly separated capture path is necessary preparation for this sequence,
not its completion. No new framework, MCU memory epic or parallel implementation

foothold; arbitrary Source authority and admitted proposal preparation remain

### 2026-11-06 — Failed Crèche cleanup still joins workers and drains the Mask

Tracing the remaining per-Play memory owners exposed a prerequisite lifetime
failure: cancellation used the ordinary poison-refusing mailbox lock before
extracting its worker, and returned immediately on a failed worker join before
attempting independent Mask retirement. A poisoned mailbox could therefore
leave a worker running; a panicked worker could skip presentation cleanup.

Cancellation now recovers poisoned state only to deactivate the encounter,
request cancellation and take its actual worker handle. It never clears poison
or reopens ordinary operation. Joining that worker and draining selected Mask
custody are both attempted, and their failures are retained together. Any fault
still returns an error without reporting successful retirement or publishing a
successful terminal receipt. The existing explicit retry can complete clean
retirement after a consumed failed join when no poison or Mask failure remains.

All 18 product Crèche tests passed, including three new actual-thread/poison
fixtures. They prove mailbox poison does not skip cancellation/join/Mask drain,
a worker panic does not skip independent Mask drain, and combined worker/Mask
failures both remain observable. Existing uncertain presentation retirement,
shared IMAGE input custody and Seed/bundle tests remain passing. Strict product
library/test Clippy passed. Logs:
`/tmp/conduit-creche-retirement-faults-final-20261106.log` and
`/tmp/conduit-creche-retirement-clippy-20261106.log`.

These are software cleanup tests, not qualification of production worker stack,
heap highwater, filesystem effects or installed IMAGE execution. They are needed
before those effects can safely relinquish their eventual memory reservation.
